<a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Infobox_Horde" title="discuss changes here"><img alt="discuss changes here" src="/images/Gossipgossipicon.png?a6d689" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="16" height="16" data-file-width="16" data-file-height="16" /></a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="noexcerpt" style="margin: calc(0.5em + 1vh) auto calc(0.5em + 1vh) min(24px, calc(15px + 1.25%)); max-width:calc(70% + 15em)">
<div style="font-style:italic">
<p>“The proud nations of the Horde are loosely joined in an alliance of convenience against a hostile world that would see them destroyed. Focused, ferocious and sometimes monstrous, the Horde values strength and honor, but struggles to keep aggression in check. Join the Horde and fight to build a world where their people can live free.”
</p>
</div>
<dl><dd>— Horde in-game's description</dd></dl></div>
<p>The <b>Horde</b> (also called the <b>New Horde</b> or <b>Thrall's Horde</b>) is one of the two major political <a href="/wiki/Faction" title="Faction">factions</a> of the mortal races in <a href="/wiki/Azeroth" title="Azeroth">Azeroth</a>, its counterpart being the <a href="/wiki/Alliance" title="Alliance">Alliance</a> which the Horde has traditionally been at war with. Driven by unity,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Playable&#95;races&#95;1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Playable_races-1"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>1<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> the Horde consists of a coalition of disparate races and cultures loosely joined in an alliance of convenience against a hostile world that would see them destroyed.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Pandaren&#95;faction&#95;selection&#95;2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pandaren_faction_selection-2"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>2<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>3<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> A faction led by off-worlders and composed of outsiders, the Horde has survived the obstacles of Azeroth by bonding together, fighting as family, comrades, or even uneasy allies.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>4<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Focused, ferocious, and sometimes monstrous, the Horde values strength and honor<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Pandaren&#95;faction&#95;selection&#95;2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pandaren_faction_selection-2"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>2<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and is relentlessly opposed to any who threaten the ideals of freedom and hope,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Playable&#95;races&#95;1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Playable_races-1"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>1<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> but struggles to keep aggression in check.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Pandaren&#95;faction&#95;selection&#95;2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pandaren_faction_selection-2"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>2<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>During <a href="/wiki/Garrosh_Hellscream" title="Garrosh Hellscream">Garrosh Hellscream</a>'s reign, the faction was plunged into a state of civil war, divided between Hellscream's pan-orc government he referred to as the "<a href="/wiki/True_Horde" title="True Horde">True Horde</a>" and the <a href="/wiki/Darkspear_Rebellion" title="Darkspear Rebellion">Darkspear Rebellion</a>, comprised of the remaining Horde races, as well as <a href="/wiki/Orc" title="Orc">orcs</a> that have decided to oppose Garrosh, under leadership of <a href="/wiki/Vol%27jin" title="Vol&#39;jin">Vol'jin</a>. After the <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Orgrimmar" title="Siege of Orgrimmar">Siege of Orgrimmar</a> upon the defeat of Garrosh, <a href="/wiki/Vol%27jin" title="Vol&#39;jin">Vol'jin</a> was chosen as the new <a href="/wiki/Warchief" title="Warchief">Warchief</a> of the Horde with every leader supporting the choice, even <a href="/wiki/Thrall" title="Thrall">Thrall</a> himself. This also marks the first time, when the Warchief's position was held by a person that isn't an orc, signifying a sense of unity between all the member races of the faction, in Vol'jin's own words - <i>The Horde is a family</i>. Following the disastrous <a href="/wiki/Battle_for_Broken_Shore" title="Battle for Broken Shore">Battle for Broken Shore</a> at the start of the <a href="/wiki/Third_invasion_of_the_Burning_Legion" title="Third invasion of the Burning Legion">third invasion of the Burning Legion</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sylvanas_Windrunner" title="Sylvanas Windrunner">Sylvanas Windrunner</a> was named the new Warchief by a dying Vol'jin, who with his last breath said she must step out of the shadows to lead.
</p><p>The Horde has throughout the <i>Warcraft</i> story remained the only force that can match up to the Alliance. These two factions have warred several times with their predecessors (<a href="/wiki/Old_Horde" title="Old Horde">Old Horde</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alliance_of_Lordaeron" title="Alliance of Lordaeron">Alliance of Lordaeron</a>) having clashed together in the <a href="/wiki/First_War" title="First War">First</a>, <a href="/wiki/Second_War" title="Second War">Second</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Third_War" title="Third War">Third Wars</a>. In the <a href="/wiki/Fourth_War" title="Fourth War">Fourth War</a>, the last major conflict as of <i><a href="/wiki/Battle_for_Azeroth" class="mw-redirect" title="Battle for Azeroth">Battle for Azeroth</a></i>, the controversial acts by the Horde <a href="/wiki/Warchief" title="Warchief">Warchief</a> <a href="/wiki/Sylvanas_Windrunner" title="Sylvanas Windrunner">Sylvanas Windrunner</a> eventually caused a Horde rebellion led by <a href="/wiki/Varok_Saurfang" title="Varok Saurfang">Varok Saurfang</a>. This led to a temporary unity between the two factions when Varok asked <a href="/wiki/Anduin_Wrynn" title="Anduin Wrynn">Anduin Wrynn</a>, High King of the Alliance, for aid in assaulting <a href="/wiki/Orgrimmar" title="Orgrimmar">Orgrimmar</a> to end both the reign of Sylvanas and the enmity between the Horde and Alliance.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Negotiation&#95;5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Negotiation-5"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>5<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Ultimately, the rebellion caused the Horde to lose its top leadership when Sylvanas <a href="/wiki/Reckoning" title="Reckoning">killed Varok and abandoned the Horde</a>. With his death and her abandonment, the Fourth War was considered over, and the Horde discarded the position of Warchief ruling the Horde in favor of a <a href="/wiki/Horde_Council" title="Horde Council">council style leadership</a>, while the two factions began talks of peace, though many surmise it may not last or cannot forgive the atrocities of the past.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Warchief&#95;of&#95;the&#95;Horde&#95;(quest)&#95;6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Warchief_of_the_Horde_(quest)-6"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>6<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>7<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Currently the Horde and Alliance are in an "uneasy armistice".<sup id="cite&#95;ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>8<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>The Horde is one of the two factions that player characters belong to―the <a href="/wiki/Alliance" title="Alliance">Alliance</a> being the other―as determined by a given character's <a href="/wiki/Race" title="Race">race</a> (with the exception of <a href="/wiki/Pandaren_(playable)" title="Pandaren (playable)">Pandaren</a> which can be both). Besides race, being a member of the Horde or Alliance affects gameplay through what <a href="/wiki/Settlement" title="Settlement">settlements</a>, <a href="/wiki/NPC" class="mw-redirect" title="NPC">NPCs</a>, <a href="/wiki/Quest" title="Quest">quests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Reputation" title="Reputation">reputation factions</a> and in some cases <a href="/wiki/Item" title="Item">items</a> and <a href="/wiki/Encounter" title="Encounter">encounters</a> the player may access. Faction mechanics also prevent Horde and Alliance players from <a href="/wiki/Language" title="Language">talking</a> with each other or making <a href="/wiki/Group" title="Group">groups</a> together, and most <a href="/wiki/PvP" class="mw-redirect" title="PvP">PvP</a> gameplay involves Horde players fighting against Alliance players. The divide between the Horde and Alliance—in regard to gameplay mechanics, story elements, and sometimes in social aspects like <a href="/wiki/Faction_pride" title="Faction pride">faction pride</a>—acts as the core element of the <i><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft" title="World of Warcraft">World of Warcraft</a></i> experience.
</p>
